<p>The <b>Zandalari Empire</b> (or the <b>Zandalar Empire</b>)<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Kezan&#39;s&#95;Taint&#95;1-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kezan&#39;s_Taint-1"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>1<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>2<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> is one of the great <a href="/wiki/Troll" title="Troll">troll</a> nations, home of the <a href="/wiki/Zandalari_troll" title="Zandalari troll">Zandalari trolls</a>, ruled by <a href="/wiki/Talanji" title="Talanji">Queen Talanji</a>. It is located on the island of <a href="/wiki/Zandalar" title="Zandalar">Zandalar</a>, formerly a mountain range where the first trolls settled around over 16,000 years ago. The most powerful tribe inhabited the highest peaks of the mountains, and called themselves Zandalari. Now the oldest-lasting <a href="/wiki/Empire" title="Empire">empire</a> on <a href="/wiki/Azeroth" title="Azeroth">Azeroth</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Faction&#95;3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Faction-3"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>3<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> having been around for at least over 16,000 years,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-BlizzCon2017&#95;4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BlizzCon2017-4"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>4<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> they possess one of the most powerful navies in existence, one that is unmatched on the <a href="/wiki/Great_Sea" title="Great Sea">Great Sea</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Faction&#95;3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Faction-3"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>3<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>

<li class="toclevel-1 tocsection-25"><a href="#Organizations"><span class="tocnumber">4</span> <span class="toctext">Organizations</span></a></li>
<li class="toclevel-1 tocsection-26"><a href="#Territories"><span class="tocnumber">5</span> <span class="toctext">Territories</span></a></li>
<li class="toclevel-1 tocsection-27"><a href="#Speculation"><span class="tocnumber">6</span> <span class="toctext">Speculation</span></a></li>
<li class="toclevel-1 tocsection-28"><a href="#References"><span class="tocnumber">7</span> <span class="toctext">References</span></a></li>
</ul>
</div>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/wiki/Zandalari_Empire?action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: History"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ancient_history">Ancient history</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/wiki/Zandalari_Empire?action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Ancient history"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:272px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Zuldazar.jpg" class="image"><img src="/images/thumb/Zuldazar.jpg/270px-Zuldazar.jpg?babb1b" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="270" height="360" class="thumbimage" data-file-width="1110" data-file-height="1479" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Zuldazar.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Zuldazar" title="Zuldazar">Zuldazar</a>, the great capital of the Zandalari.</div></div></div>
<p>The early trolls gathered around a mountain range in southern <a href="/wiki/Kalimdor_(landmass)" title="Kalimdor (landmass)">Kalimdor</a>, where many <a href="/wiki/Wild_God" title="Wild God">Wild Gods</a>, which they called <a href="/wiki/Loa" title="Loa">loa</a>, dwelled. They named the mountain range <a href="/wiki/Zandalar" title="Zandalar">Zandalar</a>, and built small encampments upon its slopes. In these old days, before trolls gathered in great cities, the <a href="/wiki/Shadow_hunter" title="Shadow hunter">shadow hunters</a> were the leaders of the small tribes. As cities grew and the Zandalari grew in power, the shadow hunters' influence was lessened, but the position is still highly respected.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>5<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Dazar" title="Dazar">Dazar</a> was the founder and first King of Zandalar, who led an exodus of his people out of the muck and mire and built a city of gold.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>6<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> It is implied that the heart of their civilization was originally <a href="/wiki/Nazmir" title="Nazmir">Nazmir</a> with the city of <a href="/wiki/Zul%27Nazman" title="Zul&#39;Nazman">Zul'Nazman</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>7<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> but they moved south into the mountains to avoid <a href="/wiki/G%27huun" title="G&#39;huun">G'huun</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>8<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> The most powerful group of trolls was called the Zandalar tribe, who claimed the Zandalar Mountains' highest plateaus. They built a small cluster of crude shrines that in time grew into the bustling temple city of <a href="/wiki/Zuldazar" title="Zuldazar">Zuldazar</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Chronicle&#95;70&#95;9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Chronicle_70-9"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>9<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> And Dazar united the tribes into the empire.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>10<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Their society was built heavily on a caste system, and as time went on those castes evolved into their own people. Most Zandalari were scholarly and valued knowledge, but a significant portion desired conquest instead. Over the next several centuries, these disaffected trolls left the Zandalari to found other tribes. The scholarly Zandalari were content to see them go, they saw them as children who would see the folly of youthful rebellion and come back begging for Zandalari approval. However, the trolls would not return.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-SotH200&#95;11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SotH200-11"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>11<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Two main <a href="/wiki/Empire" title="Empire">empires</a> were the <a href="/wiki/Forest_troll" title="Forest troll">forest troll</a> nation of the <a href="/wiki/Amani_Empire" title="Amani Empire">Amani</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Jungle_troll" title="Jungle troll">jungle troll</a> nation of the <a href="/wiki/Gurubashi_Empire" title="Gurubashi Empire">Gurubashi</a>, who challenged one another for territory and power. Other empires co-existed with them, whose focus was not to climb any further in power at the time: the <a href="/wiki/Ice_troll" title="Ice troll">ice troll</a> nation of the <a href="/wiki/Drakkari_Empire" title="Drakkari Empire">Drakkari</a> and the progenitor nation of them all, the Zandalari.
</p><p>As time went on, other trolls came to regard the Zandalari as an overarching priest caste for all trolls. The Zandalari preserved troll history, and they worked to further the goals of troll society as a whole. Though they had the respect and admiration of all other trolls, the Zandalari did not wield sovereignty over the other trolls, nor did they seek such authority.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Compendium&#95;12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Compendium-12"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>12<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div style="clear:both; margin: 0; padding: 0"></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="The_Aqir_and_Troll_War">The Aqir and Troll War</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/wiki/Zandalari_Empire?action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: The Aqir and Troll War"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<dl><dd><span class="noexcerpt" data-nosnippet=""><i>Main article: <a href="/wiki/Aqir_and_Troll_War" title="Aqir and Troll War">Aqir and Troll War</a></i></span></dd></dl>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:322px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Kalimdor_Chronicle.jpg" class="image"><img src="/images/thumb/Kalimdor_Chronicle.jpg/320px-Kalimdor_Chronicle.jpg?5bf25f" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="320" height="213" class="thumbimage" data-file-width="5400" data-file-height="3600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Kalimdor_Chronicle.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Aftermath of the <a href="/wiki/Aqir_and_Troll_War" title="Aqir and Troll War">Aqir and Troll War</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>Only one place was forbidden by Zandalari's witch doctors and priests: a small mound of blackened stone at the base of the Zandalar Mountains. The loa warned the tribes' mystics of severe consequences should they disturb the black stones. For many years, none of the trolls dared disobey. But curiosity eventually won out, and 16,000 years ago<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Chronicle&#95;70&#95;9-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Chronicle_70-9"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>9<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> a group of rebellious trolls led by the Gurubashi exile <a href="/wiki/Zan%27do" title="Zan&#39;do">Zan'do</a> plumbed the forbidden mound. They discovered that the black stones were not mere rocks - they were the jagged hide of a monstrous creature. The trolls believed it to be an undiscovered loa, and a powerful one at that, considering that the other loa were frightened by it. The trolls performed vile rituals and living sacrifices to awaken the slumbering monstrosity.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>13<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Roused by the blood offerings, the gigantic <a href="/wiki/C%27Thrax" title="C&#39;Thrax">C'Thraxxi</a> <a href="/wiki/General_(rank)" title="General (rank)">general</a> known as <a href="/wiki/Kith%27ix" title="Kith&#39;ix">Kith'ix</a> emerged from its long slumber and slaughtered all those who had revived it. The ancient loa had in fact buried Kith'ix in ancient times when it collapsed before the Zandalar Mountains, fleeing, gravely wounded, from the fight it had with <a href="/wiki/Keeper" title="Keeper">Keeper</a> <a href="/wiki/Tyr" title="Tyr">Tyr</a>. Kith'ix, fully awakened, knew it would delight the <a href="/wiki/Old_God" title="Old God">Old Gods</a> to see this pitiful civilization rent to ashes. It reached out with its mind and took control of the <a href="/wiki/Aqir" title="Aqir">aqir</a> dwelling underground. The aqiri swarms began building a vast subterranean <a href="/wiki/Empire" title="Empire">empire</a> known as <a href="/wiki/Azj%27Aqir" title="Azj&#39;Aqir">Azj'Aqir</a>, and when they had swollen enough in number, the C'Thrax led them to make war against the trolls.
</p><p>The aqiri threat was unlike any the trolls had ever faced. Numerous smaller tribes fell before them. As they got dangerously close to the Zandalar Mountains, the Zandalari moved to act. They united the disparate troll tribes into a single mighty force, which they called the <a href="/wiki/Empire_of_Zul" title="Empire of Zul">Empire of Zul</a>. Its members would put aside their differences and work together to destroy the aqir.
</p><p>The Zandalari took on the role of commanding the troll armies. With their temple city looming high in the mountains, they could detect enemy movements and direct attacks at weak points. Under their guidance, the other trolls used ambush tactics to whittle down the enemy's numbers in the surrounding jungles. Elsewhere, revered priests summoned the loa to assail their enemies, and they would join the troll warriors in battle, ripping through the aqiri ranks and even wounding Kith'ix.
</p><p>The aqir were forced to retreat before they could mount a proper siege on the sacred mountains. Kith'ix, gravely wounded by the loa, fled to the northeast. The Zandalari knew that their enemy still posed a grave threat, as it would attack outlying troll territories. At the Zandalari's behest, the other tribes moved out to hunt down the aqir. The trolls quickly learned it was not enough to simply kill the insectoids: if any aqir escaped underground, they would establish a new colony and rise up again later. To permanently end the threat, no corner of the continent could be left unguarded. The Zandalari thus convinced the most power-hungry troll factions to establish new strongholds across Azeroth. Chief among these groups were the Amani, Gurubashi, and Drakkari. After defeating the aqir, they could claim the fertile new lands for themselves, without any competition.
</p><p>And so the Drakkari pushed into the frigid north, but they faced unexpected resistance in the form of corrupted tol'vir, who had been enthralled by the aqir. The <a href="/wiki/Obsidian_destroyer" title="Obsidian destroyer">obsidian destroyers</a> almost overpowered the Drakkari, but they devised brilliant ways to topple and shatter their foes.
</p><p>The Gurubashi also encountered corrupted titan-forged in the southwest, where the aqir had overrun <a href="/wiki/Ahn%27Qiraj" title="Ahn&#39;Qiraj">Ahn'Qiraj</a>, in the form of enslaved <a href="/wiki/Anubisath" title="Anubisath">anubisath</a> giants. They slaughtered several large Gurubashi encampments. The Zandalari instructed Gurubashi priests to separate their tribe into smaller, more mobile raiding groups instead of large armies, allowing them to bleed their enemies dry and win.
</p><p>Meanwhile, the Amani had set out to destroy Kith'ix and tracked its trail to the northeastern woodlands. In a suicidal attack against the C'Thrax, they defeated Kith'ix, and only a tiny fraction of the troll army survived. They became legend among the trolls and established a temple city known as <a href="/wiki/Zul%27Aman" title="Zul&#39;Aman">Zul'Aman</a>.
</p><p>After many centuries of brutal fighting, the trolls shattered the aqir empire. Without war to bind them together, the troll factions grew ever more distant and insular. The tribes eventually grew into empires in their own right, and the Zandalari withdrew to their mountain plateaus to pursue spiritual knowledge, but they would always maintain an immense influence of the disparate troll societies.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>14<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="The_mogu_and_pandaren">The mogu and pandaren</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/wiki/Zandalari_Empire?action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: The mogu and pandaren"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:322px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Chronicle_Pandaren_v_Zandalari.jpg" class="image"><img src="/images/thumb/Chronicle_Pandaren_v_Zandalari.jpg/320px-Chronicle_Pandaren_v_Zandalari.jpg?88b9f0" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="320" height="425" class="thumbimage" data-file-width="1114" data-file-height="1481" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Chronicle_Pandaren_v_Zandalari.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A <a href="/wiki/Zandalari_troll" title="Zandalari troll">Zandalari troll</a> on a <a href="/wiki/Raptor" title="Raptor">raptor</a> fighting a <a href="/wiki/Pandaren" title="Pandaren">pandaren</a> on a <a href="/wiki/Cloud_serpent" title="Cloud serpent">cloud serpent</a> during the <a href="/wiki/Zandalari_Troll_Wars" title="Zandalari Troll Wars">Zandalari Troll Wars</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>Between 15,000 and 12,000 years ago,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>15<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Zulathra" title="Zulathra">Zulathra</a>, a high priest of the Zandalari, proposed to the <a href="/wiki/Mogu" title="Mogu">mogu</a> Emperor <a href="/wiki/Lei_Shen" title="Lei Shen">Lei Shen</a> an exchange of each other's secrets; the mogu's <a href="/wiki/Arcane" title="Arcane">arcane</a> power for the trolls' knowledge of the lands beyond the vale. Together, the two <a href="/wiki/Empire" title="Empire">empires</a> would be unstoppable.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>16<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> They should have destroyed each other, but they understood that in a war, pitting strength against strength, the survivor would be weakened and might even succumb to creatures far weaker than it. That would be a tragedy that neither race wanted. With back firmly set against back, mogu and trolls carved out their positions in the world.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>17<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Both the mogu and Zandalari held contempt for one another. The Zandalari thought themselves above the mogu for two reasons: the presence of the <a href="/wiki/Loa" title="Loa">loa</a>, and that the Zandalari did not use slaves as the mogu did. The mutual contempt between the two would grow to polite indifference. They trusted the other not to attack because they were confident they would defeat the other. While Zandalari and mogu stood back to back, they did not watch the other and did not see the other falter.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-SotH200&#95;11-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SotH200-11"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>11<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>12,200 years before the <a href="/wiki/Dark_Portal" title="Dark Portal">Dark Portal</a>, Lei Shen sought to claim the <a href="/wiki/Forge_of_Origination" title="Forge of Origination">Forge of Origination</a> in <a href="/wiki/Uldum" title="Uldum">Uldum</a>. Its <a href="/wiki/Tol%27vir" title="Tol&#39;vir">tol'vir</a> guardians were to become honored members of the mogu empire under his leadership. However, the tol'vir became furious upon learning that Lei Shen had betrayed <a href="/wiki/Highkeeper_Ra" class="mw-redirect" title="Highkeeper Ra">Highkeeper Ra</a> and refused. The mogu army marched on Uldum accompanied by their troll allies. The tol'vir could not resist them and instead configured the Forge of Origination to purge the area around Uldum of almost all life. The death of the Thunder King and most of the Zandalari leadership (including Zulathra, whose life had been artificially extended by Lei Shen) left massive power vacuums, striking a mortal blow to both empires.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Chron85&#95;18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Chron85-18"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>18<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>300 years later, after the <a href="/wiki/Pandaren" title="Pandaren">pandaren</a> revolted against the mogu, a great debate raged within the Zandalari capital of Zuldazar as to how to acquire a large plot of land that the Zandalari had been promised by their mogu allies. A descendant of the great high priest Zulathra, <a href="/wiki/Mengazi" title="Mengazi">Mengazi</a>, convinced the other Zandalari that the best course of action was to take the territory by force, as the pandaren and their allies were unlikely to honor an agreement made by their cruel former masters. And so the Zandalari marched south into <a href="/wiki/Pandaria" title="Pandaria">Pandaria</a>, beginning the <a href="/wiki/Zandalari_Troll_Wars" title="Zandalari Troll Wars">Zandalari Troll Wars</a>.
</p><p>Overcoming this threat was to be one of the <a href="/wiki/Pandaren_Empire" title="Pandaren Empire">Pandaren Empire</a>'s first tests of strength,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-ZandalariMogu&#95;19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ZandalariMogu-19"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>19<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> but through tenacity, diplomacy, and a unique form of unarmed combat, the pandaren staged a successful revolution that deposed their tyrants and established an empire that would prosper for thousands of years. Imbued with mystic powers and riding great dinosaur war mounts, the Zandalari at first met with little resistance by the pandaren. The tide of war only turned when <a href="/wiki/Jiang" title="Jiang">Jiang</a> formed the <a href="/wiki/Order_of_the_Cloud_Serpent" title="Order of the Cloud Serpent">Order of the Cloud Serpent</a> by teaching her fellow pandaren how to tame and ride the powerful <a href="/wiki/Cloud_serpent" title="Cloud serpent">cloud serpents</a>, thus giving the pandaren a counter to the Zandalari's airborne <a href="/wiki/Pterrordax" title="Pterrordax">pterrorwings</a> and <a href="/wiki/Bat" title="Bat">batriders</a>. The trolls realized they could not win by conventional means, so Mengazi turned to one final tactic: resurrecting Lei Shen. However, the trolls' efforts to resurrect the Thunder King from his resting place in the <a href="/wiki/Tomb_of_Conquerors" title="Tomb of Conquerors">Tomb of Conquerors</a> were thwarted by the serpent riders, as Jiang sacrificed herself in a final, desperate attack, killing Mengazi. The other Zandalari soon broke ranks and fled back to Zandalar in shame, ending the war. Despite being defeated and pushed from the island now called Pandaria, the Zandalari, just like the mogu, remained in Pandaren lore, and slowly turned into "bogeymen".<sup id="cite&#95;ref-ZandalariMogu&#95;19-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ZandalariMogu-19"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>19<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Taran_Zhu" title="Taran Zhu">Taran Zhu</a> theorized that humans and possibly even some Zandalari may have sided with the Pandaren in the <a href="/wiki/Pandaren_revolution" class="mw-redirect" title="Pandaren revolution">pandaren rebellion</a> 12,000 years ago. When Vol'jin pointed out the attitude of the Zandalari and the uncivilized humans at that time, Taran Zhu explained that there are some exceptions in every race.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>20<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="The_night_elves_and_the_Sundering">The night elves and the Sundering</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/wiki/Zandalari_Empire?action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: The night elves and the Sundering"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In the millennia that followed the founding of the empire, the troll race continued to maintain control over much of the continent of Ancient Kalimdor. This changed, however, when a new race known as the <a href="/wiki/Kaldorei" class="mw-redirect" title="Kaldorei">kaldorei</a> emerged near the waters of the mystical <a href="/wiki/Well_of_Eternity" title="Well of Eternity">Well of Eternity</a>. Although originally evolved from trolls (greatly influenced by their proximity to the well) the kaldorei, or night elves, bore very little resemblance at all to the other ancient race. Physically, they were much more sleek and elegant, but culturally they had adopted their own traditions. They adopted new religious beliefs and quickly established their own civilization in the center of Kalimdor. The night elves, highly intelligent and able to harness the innate magical forces of the world, began to forge their own empire completely independent of their troll ancestors.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>21<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Despite the size and glory of the mighty Empire of Zul, despite its vast temple cities and rich culture, it was utterly eclipsed by the rapid expansion and development of the glorious <a href="/wiki/Kaldorei_Empire" title="Kaldorei Empire">Kaldorei Empire</a>. In an ensuing golden age, the night elves expanded across the entire continent, driving the other races, including the trolls, farther away. At this point, the Empire of Zul was a shadow of its former self, devoid of the unity and energy it possessed when it warred with the aqir. Eventually, under the fantastic reign of the kaldorei monarch known as <a href="/wiki/Queen_Azshara" title="Queen Azshara">Queen Azshara</a>, the two empires came into direct conflict. Of all the threats that the kaldorei encountered during the expansion of their empire, only the trolls had drawn their full attention.
</p><p>Sporadic battles soon broke out between the two sides in the south and although what remained of the Empire of Zul fought ferociously to defend their lands, they were simply unable to resist the power of the night elves' sorcery. Their armies buckled under the onslaught and they found themselves unable to prevail against their elven foes. Azshara, however, had no interest in controlling the trolls' lands and so an accord was reached between the kaldorei and the Zandalari tribe, ever the leaders of the troll race. In exchange for a strict end to all troll incursions and expansion near night elf territory, they would—by Azshara's grace—be permitted to remain in possession of their sacred mountain ranges to the south. Though the terms were extremely uneven and humiliating for the trolls, they had no choice but to acquiesce. The shame of this agreement would sow the seeds of resentment among all trolls for thousands of years to come. Even in the modern day, the inherent troll resentment of elves can be attributed to these events.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>22<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>When the <a href="/wiki/Great_Sundering" title="Great Sundering">Great Sundering</a> came, the wise Zandalari trolls wove swift magics to protect their realm. The land around their territory shuddered; waves crashed in and trees and soil sank beneath the waves. Through it all, the Zandalari's land remained largely intact. After the Sundering, the Zandalari discovered that their territory was now an island. Seeing no reason to leave it, they set about rebuilding their settlements and continuing their studies.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Compendium&#95;12-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Compendium-12"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>12<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> They adapted to island life, and over thousands of years, learned how to master the seas.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-BlizzCon2017&#95;4-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BlizzCon2017-4"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>4<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>After the Sundering, the Zandalari started exploring numerous islands that dotted the newly formed <a href="/wiki/Great_Sea" title="Great Sea">Great Sea</a>. During one of these trips, they discovered <a href="/wiki/Kezan" title="Kezan">Kezan</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Goblin" title="Goblin">goblin</a> race, who were very primitive but still possessed some intellect. Initially, the two races kept a distance as the Zandalari only came to the island in order to mine the rare mineral <a href="/wiki/Kaja%27mite" title="Kaja&#39;mite">kaja'mite</a>. While the trolls didn't know too much about it, they realized that it was very potent, and used it as a sacred component in their rituals and ceremonies. For many centuries the trolls mined and occasionally even paid goblins in trinkets in order to work for them, but this arrangement was soon to change. At some point the trolls, while digging, discovered that Kezan possessed an unimaginable deposit of kaja'mite underground, more than they would ever need. So rather than digging themselves, they enslaved the goblins and forced them to drill in terrible conditions. For thousands of years the goblins, too weak to resist, suffered under the rule of the Zandalari,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-WoWCV1&#95;158&#95;23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WoWCV1_158-23"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>23<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> and the island was considered a part of the Zandalar Empire.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Kezan&#39;s&#95;Taint&#95;1-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kezan&#39;s_Taint-1"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>1<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="The_Council_of_Tribes">The Council of Tribes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/wiki/Zandalari_Empire?action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: The Council of Tribes"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>There was once a time when no single emperor could rule Zandalar. After a period of civil war, the heads of three families formed an uneasy coalition and tried to rule the empire together: the <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Tribes" title="Council of Tribes">Council of Tribes</a>. Though they did not often agree, there was peace in Zandalar. Briefly.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>24<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>During the final era of the Council of Tribes, a <a href="/wiki/Plague_of_corrupted_blood" title="Plague of corrupted blood">blood plague</a> broke out, when a foolish troll tried to summon <a href="/wiki/Hakkar_the_Soulflayer" title="Hakkar the Soulflayer">Hakkar the Soulflayer</a> to the island of <a href="/wiki/Zandalar" title="Zandalar">Zandalar</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>25<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> killing thousands of trolls. It was deemed that the bodies, too polluted for proper burial, would instead be handled by golems, burned, and kept in special urns. As time progressed, this method became commonplace among the Zandalari in disposing of their dead.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>26<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> This marked the end of the caste whose purpose was to handle and protect the remains of Zandalar's former rulers.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>27<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>After the great plague, there were still those who clung to the magics of the <a href="/wiki/G%27huun" title="G&#39;huun">blood god</a>. They tested their magics and perfected them. One such relic was a totem, which when concentrated upon, would drain not just the life from their enemies, but also their blood, infusing the caster with it and altering them in unusual ways. This was the dawn of who the Zandalari call, "the <a href="/wiki/Blood_troll" title="Blood troll">blood trolls</a>".<sup id="cite&#95;ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>28<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="The_Troll_Wars">The Troll Wars</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/wiki/Zandalari_Empire?action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: The Troll Wars"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Thousands of years after the Sundering, 2,800 years before the <a href="/wiki/Dark_Portal" title="Dark Portal">Dark Portal</a> was opened, the Zandalari traveled to the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Kingdoms" title="Eastern Kingdoms">Eastern Kingdoms</a> to help the <a href="/wiki/Forest_troll" title="Forest troll">forest trolls</a> of Zul'Aman against the <a href="/wiki/High_elf" title="High elf">high elves</a> of <a href="/wiki/Quel%27Thalas" title="Quel&#39;Thalas">Quel'Thalas</a>. In the Amani, the Zandalari saw an opportunity to revitalize one of their race's most powerful tribes and reassert dominance in the Eastern Kingdoms.
</p><p>The Zandalari formed an alliance with the Amani and marshaled their forces for the impending conquest. The Zandalari would ensure that the mighty loa demigods would aid the trolls in battle. To settle the matter of leadership and infighting that had plagued the forest trolls, the Zandalari made <a href="/wiki/Jintha" title="Jintha">Jintha</a>, one of the Amani's fiercest warriors and descendant of the original patriarch of the <a href="/wiki/Amani_tribe" title="Amani tribe">Amani tribe</a> <a href="/wiki/Manzajin?action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Manzajin (page does not exist)" rel="nofollow">Manzajin</a>, the ruler of his people after the previous ruler <a href="/wiki/Tan%27dani?action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Tan&#39;dani (page does not exist)" rel="nofollow">Tan'dani</a> disappeared while going to meet with the Zandalari.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>29<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Small Amani warbands ventured from Zul'Aman to raid the high elves and test their strength. The cunning trolls hid their true numbers and capabilities; and after several successful attacks, decided that the time for all-out war had finally come. Without warning, tens of thousands of troll fighters exploded from the shadowy forests. Fearsome loa demigods marched alongside the Amani, infusing their adherents with supernatural power. The high elves struggled desperately to hold back the trolls but were forced to give ground. With astonishing speed, the Amani laid waste to the outer reaches of Quel'Thalas. The Zandalari emissaries were pleased by what they saw: even the elves and their potent arcane powers could not withstand the might of the Amani—the might of the troll race.
</p><p>Eventually, the elves would find help in the <a href="/wiki/Human" title="Human">humans</a>, whose tribes had been united into the kingdom of <a href="/wiki/Arathor" title="Arathor">Arathor</a> by <a href="/wiki/Thoradin" title="Thoradin">Thoradin</a>. The union of elves and humans ultimately ended in the trolls losing what would come to be known as the <a href="/wiki/Troll_Wars" title="Troll Wars">Troll Wars</a>, with Jintha and hundreds of troll soldiers dying to a horrific firestorm conjured by the <a href="/wiki/One_Hundred" title="One Hundred">One Hundred</a>, the first human mages in history. The disastrous battle floored the Zandalari emissaries. Once so confident of victory, they skulked back to their island home in disbelief and shame. For them, the defeat marked a dark turning point in troll history, one from which their beleaguered race might never recover.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>30<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="First_war_against_the_Soulflayer">First war against the Soulflayer</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/wiki/Zandalari_Empire?action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: First war against the Soulflayer"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>1,500 years before the opening of the <a href="/wiki/Dark_Portal" title="Dark Portal">Dark Portal</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>31<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> the Zandalari noticed that their Gurubashi brethren on the mainland were growing rapidly in power and territory, attributing their victories to the influence of the god <a href="/wiki/Hakkar_the_Soulflayer" title="Hakkar the Soulflayer">Hakkar the Soulflayer</a>. At first, the Zandalari were glad for their fellow trolls. However, as <a href="/wiki/Zul%27Gurub" title="Zul&#39;Gurub">Zul'Gurub</a> continued to expand, the Zandalari's pleased surprise turned to disquiet. They consulted myths and delved into historical accounts. What they learned horrified them: Hakkar was also known as the "Soulflayer", a name he had richly earned. He filled his adherents with murderous rage and reveled in their dark emotions. The carnage that invariably followed Hakkar suited him perfectly, for having consumed blood he had developed a taste for it. Worse, he became more powerful as he consumed the bloodshed in his name, and his tie to the world that was feeding him grew stronger.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Compendium&#95;12-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Compendium-12"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>12<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Every Zandalari who could fight immediately set out for <a href="/wiki/Stranglethorn_Vale" title="Stranglethorn Vale">Stranglethorn Vale</a> and brought the terrible tidings to the jungle trolls. They were branded as heretics. Heathens, known as <a href="/wiki/Freethinker" title="Freethinker">freethinkers</a>, dared to challenge the <a href="/wiki/Hakkari" title="Hakkari">priests</a> of Hakkar and were therefore hunted and killed as vermin. However, those heathens never wavered in their fight against the Blood God.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>32<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> The Gurubashi Empire was already in a state of civil unrest due to the numerous sacrifices that Hakkar was demanding. With the Zandalar tribe's news, the jungle trolls banded together with the Zandalar tribe and rose up in open revolt against the bloodthirsty Soulflayer. The united tribes narrowly defeated the Blood God and cast out the <a href="/wiki/Atal%27ai" class="mw-redirect" title="Atal&#39;ai">Atal'ai</a>. Despite their victory, however, the Gurubashi Empire soon fell.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Compendium&#95;12-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Compendium-12"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>12<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Shortly after the fall of the Gurubashi, the Zandalar almost succumbed to a final defeat from evil within their own tribe. It was then that the <a href="/wiki/Confessor" title="Confessor">confessors</a> amongst them arose, preserving the tribe by banishing out those who would destroy their own kind.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Confessor&#39;sBindings&#95;33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Confessor&#39;sBindings-33"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>33<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> In the dark days after the Gurubashi Empire ripped itself up, the <a href="/wiki/Augur" title="Augur">augurs</a> led the Zandalari to the tribe's safe haven in the <a href="/wiki/South_Seas" title="South Seas">South Seas</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Augur&#39;sBelt&#95;34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Augur&#39;sBelt-34"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>34<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> In those dark times, Zandalar <a href="/wiki/Predator_(Zandalari)" title="Predator (Zandalari)">predators</a> used their prowess to provide sustenance for the tribe.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Predator&#39;sBracers&#95;35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Predator&#39;sBracers-35"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>35<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="The_goblin_rebellion">The goblin rebellion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/wiki/Zandalari_Empire?action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: The goblin rebellion"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>As the trolls' goblin slaves worked the kaja'mite mines on Kezan, they breathed in the dust of the ore that clouded the mines, over time it began to awake the goblins' dormant intelligence. Secretly they plotted to start a rebellion, gathering any materials they could find and hide in order to create a wide range of weapons, traps, and explosives. One hundred years before the opening of the <a href="/wiki/Dark_Portal" title="Dark Portal">Dark Portal</a>, the troll overseers were caught completely off guard when the goblin masses stormed out from the mines, armed with technology that the Zandalari couldn't even imagine at the time. The trolls' hold over Kezan was shattered completely, their mining operation failed and the surviving Zandalari fled. The goblins were now free, and in order to celebrate their newfound freedom they turned on each other, creating chaos as countless feuds were waged and alliances formed. Soon enough they were split into various different groups, with the most powerful of these groups known as the <a href="/wiki/Cartel" title="Cartel">cartels</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-WoWCV1&#95;158&#95;23-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WoWCV1_158-23"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>23<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Although they no longer inhabit it, Kezan is still sacred to the Zandalari to this day.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Kezan&#39;s&#95;Taint&#95;1-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kezan&#39;s_Taint-1"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>1<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Wrath_of_the_Soulflayer">Wrath of the Soulflayer</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/wiki/Zandalari_Empire?action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Wrath of the Soulflayer"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div style="clear:left;"></div><p><span style="display:inline-block; min-width: 32px; text-align:center"><a href="/wiki/World_of_Warcraft" title="World of Warcraft"><img alt="World of Warcraft" src="/images/WoW_Icon_update.png?d4be1e" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="32" height="16" style="vertical-align: middle" data-file-width="32" data-file-height="16" /></a></span> <span class="greybar" style="font-size: 0.85em; padding: 0 0.2em;"><b>This section concerns content related to the original <i><a href="/wiki/World_of_Warcraft" title="World of Warcraft">World of Warcraft</a></i>.</b></span></p><div style="clear:left;"></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:322px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Yojamba_Isle.jpg" class="image"><img src="/images/thumb/Yojamba_Isle.jpg/320px-Yojamba_Isle.jpg?4fbb7f" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="320" height="240" class="thumbimage" data-file-width="700" data-file-height="525" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Yojamba_Isle.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Yojamba_Isle" title="Yojamba Isle">Yojamba Isle</a> in <a href="/wiki/Northern_Stranglethorn" title="Northern Stranglethorn">Northern Stranglethorn</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>The Zandalari were shocked to learn of the attempt to summon Hakkar in the <a href="/wiki/Sunken_Temple" class="mw-redirect" title="Sunken Temple">Sunken Temple</a> and believed the loa's return would herald a dark time for trolls. They were pleased to hear that the Atal'ai had been defeated in the temple by the <a href="/wiki/Alliance" title="Alliance">Alliance</a>, but their relief soon turned to horror. Some of Hakkar's adherents survived, and they were continuing their quest.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Chronicle3&#95;36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Chronicle3-36"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>36<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> In time, the exiled Atal'ai priests have discovered that Hakkar's physical form can only be summoned within the ancient and once-deserted capital of the Gurubashi Empire, <a href="/wiki/Zul%27Gurub" title="Zul&#39;Gurub">Zul'Gurub</a>. Unfortunately, the priests have met with success in their quest to call forth Hakkar — reports confirm the presence of the dreaded Soulflayer in the heart of the ruins.
</p><p>And so the Zandalar tribe arrived on the shores of Azeroth at <a href="/wiki/Yojamba_Isle" title="Yojamba Isle">Yojamba Isle</a> to battle Hakkar once again. But the Blood God had grown increasingly powerful, bending several tribes to his will and even commanded the avatars of the Gurubashi's loa — <a href="/wiki/Hir%27eek" title="Hir&#39;eek">Bat</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bethekk" title="Bethekk">Panther</a>, <a href="/wiki/Shirvallah" title="Shirvallah">Tiger</a>, <a href="/wiki/Shadra" title="Shadra">Spider</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Hethiss" title="Hethiss">Snake</a>. With the tribes splintered, the Zandalari were forced to recruit champions from <a href="/wiki/Azeroth" title="Azeroth">Azeroth</a>'s varied and disparate races to battle the Soulflayer.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>37<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> The champions and their Primal God aspects fed the awesome power of the Soulflayer. Adventurers brave enough to venture into the foreboding ruins had to overcome the High Priests if they were to have any hope of confronting the mighty blood god.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>38<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>The Zandalari knew that they did not have the resources to scour the Atal'ai and their leader, <a href="/wiki/Jin%27do_the_Hexxer" title="Jin&#39;do the Hexxer">Jin'do the Hexxer</a>, from Zul'Gurub. They sent word through the <a href="/wiki/Darkspear_tribe" title="Darkspear tribe">Darkspear tribe</a> that the <a href="/wiki/Horde" title="Horde">Horde</a> needed to act immediately for Hakkar being successfully summoned would throw the world into chaos. A large Horde strike force was sent to Zul'Gurub in turn.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Chronicle3&#95;36-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Chronicle3-36"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>36<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> After the Soulflayer was defeated, <a href="/wiki/Heart_of_Hakkar" title="Heart of Hakkar">his cursed heart</a>, the still-burning kernel of Hakkar's power, was brought to <a href="/wiki/Molthor" title="Molthor">Molthor</a> in the hope the Zandalari would know how to destroy it. The heart was banished.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>39<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Wrath_of_the_Lich_King">Wrath of the Lich King</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/wiki/Zandalari_Empire?action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Wrath of the Lich King"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div style="clear:left;"></div><p><span style="display:inline-block; min-width: 32px; text-align:center"><a href="/wiki/World_of_Warcraft:_Wrath_of_the_Lich_King" title="Wrath of the Lich King"><img alt="Wrath of the Lich King" src="/images/Wrath-Logo-Small.png?6705fd" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="36" height="19" style="vertical-align: middle" data-file-width="36" data-file-height="19" /></a></span> <span class="greybar" style="font-size: 0.85em; padding: 0 0.2em;"><b>This section concerns content related to <i><a href="/wiki/World_of_Warcraft:_Wrath_of_the_Lich_King" title="World of Warcraft: Wrath of the Lich King">Wrath of the Lich King</a></i>.</b></span></p><div style="clear:left;"></div>
<p>During the <a href="/wiki/War_against_the_Lich_King" title="War against the Lich King">war against the Lich King</a>, Zandalari trolls traveled to <a href="/wiki/Zul%27Drak" title="Zul&#39;Drak">Zul'Drak</a> in <a href="/wiki/Northrend" title="Northrend">Northrend</a>, not to help their cousins but to bear witness and record the fall of the <a href="/wiki/Drakkari_Empire" title="Drakkari Empire">Drakkari Empire</a> to the <a href="/wiki/Scourge" title="Scourge">Scourge</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>40<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>After they established bases at <a href="/wiki/Zim%27Torga" title="Zim&#39;Torga">Zim'Torga</a> and <a href="/wiki/Dubra%27Jin" title="Dubra&#39;Jin">Dubra'Jin</a>, they discovered that the Drakkari were killing their loa to obtain their powers. This was something new; in the recent conflicts at Zul'Aman and Zul'Gurub, the loa had been imprisoned, not outright killed. The Zandalari opposed the Drakkari in their efforts to slay their gods, but still did not help them against the Scourge.
</p><p>Among the Zandalari in Zul'Drak was <a href="/wiki/Zul" title="Zul">Zul</a>, a prophet of the <a href="/wiki/Zanchuli_Council" title="Zanchuli Council">Zanchuli Council</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>41<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cataclysm">Cataclysm</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/wiki/Zandalari_Empire?action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Cataclysm"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div style="clear:left;"></div><p><span style="display:inline-block; min-width: 32px; text-align:center"><a href="/wiki/World_of_Warcraft:_Cataclysm" title="Cataclysm"><img alt="Cataclysm" src="/images/Cata-Logo-Small.png?cd16d8" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="39" height="15" style="vertical-align: middle" data-file-width="39" data-file-height="15" /></a></span> <span class="greybar" style="font-size: 0.85em; padding: 0 0.2em;"><b>This section concerns content related to <i><a href="/wiki/World_of_Warcraft:_Cataclysm" title="World of Warcraft: Cataclysm">Cataclysm</a></i>.</b></span></p><div style="clear:left;"></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:322px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Zandalar_troll_meeting.jpg" class="image"><img src="/images/thumb/Zandalar_troll_meeting.jpg/320px-Zandalar_troll_meeting.jpg?f388f6" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="320" height="180" class="thumbimage" data-file-width="1920" data-file-height="1080" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Zandalar_troll_meeting.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Meeting between <a href="/wiki/Zul" title="Zul">Zul</a> and the troll tribes of <a href="/wiki/Azeroth" title="Azeroth">Azeroth</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>With the Gurubashi trolls and Hakkar no longer an issue, most of the Zandalari in Stranglethorn Vale returned to Zandalar, leaving <a href="/wiki/Yojamba_Isle" title="Yojamba Isle">Yojamba Isle</a> empty.
</p><p>Years ago, <a href="/wiki/Rastakhan" title="Rastakhan">God King Rastakhan</a>'s most trusted prophet, <a href="/wiki/Zul" title="Zul">Zul</a>, warned him of <a href="/wiki/Shattering" class="mw-redirect" title="Shattering">a terrible Cataclysm</a>. For Zul had seen a vision of a <a href="/wiki/Deathwing" title="Deathwing">great armored dragon</a> clenching the world in his ferocious jaws. Months later Zul returned, bearing more grim news from his visions. He saw a <a href="/wiki/Naga" title="Naga">legion of serpents</a> pouring forth from a gaping fissure that tore open the floor of the ocean. Still, King Rastakhan did nothing. Finally, mere months before the Cataclysm, Zul returned. Tearing his clothes and throwing his staff to the ground, Zul spoke of earthquakes and tidal waves. He described the golden capital of Zandalar slowly sinking beneath the waves in the aftermath of the Cataclysm, its once-great people drowning as their mighty works slipped forever beneath the sea.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-The&#95;Zandalari&#95;Prophecy&#95;42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Zandalari_Prophecy-42"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>42<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> He advised the council and the king to unite with the other troll tribes and to abandon their doomed homeland. Despite Zul's infamy, the council refused to believe in the scope of the disaster to come. Many felt that Zul was grandstanding to increase his own status and power.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>43<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> But the Cataclysm would come just as Zul foresaw and Zandalar would indeed be damaged; the spine of the land split in two,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-The&#95;Zandalari&#95;Prophecy&#95;42-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Zandalari_Prophecy-42"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>42<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> parts of its capital fell into the sea,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>44<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> and the region of <a href="/wiki/Nazmir" title="Nazmir">Nazmir</a> began to sink.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-BattleContinents&#95;45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BattleContinents-45"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>45<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> But though Zandalar was indeed damaged, it did not sink as Zul predicted. While Zul and the Golden Fleet left Zandalar, those that stayed behind endured and rebuilt.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>46<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>In years past, the Zandalar tribe, encompassing the wise and scholarly progenitors of the trolls, heroically assisted the Horde and the Alliance with thwarting the nefarious activities of their brethren in Zul'Gurub and Zul'Drak. Yet the chaos unleashed by the recent Cataclysm has reshaped the Zandalari's philosophy about the world and the dwindling remnants of their race. Led by Zul, the Zandalari have issued a call to Azeroth's embattled troll populations: unite into a single mighty empire and save their race from extinction. Thus far, the Gurubashi and Amani trolls have answered the summons and received aid from the Zandalari, allowing them to rebuild their respective capitals in Zul'Gurub and Zul'Aman. Not all trolls, however, have so eagerly adopted the Zandalari's grandiose vision of a new troll empire. Vol'jin and his Darkspear tribe are opposed to the warmongering espoused by Zul. To prevent the burgeoning Gurubashi and Amani troll forces from wreaking havoc on Azeroth, Vol'jin has reached out to his Horde comrades and members of the Alliance for assistance. Should the Darkspears' campaign fail, a new troll empire shall rise and bring war to a world already beset by suffering and conflict.
</p><p>The Zandalar tribe <a href="/wiki/War_against_the_Zandalari" title="War against the Zandalari">returned</a>, resulting in the <a href="/wiki/Gurubashi_tribe" title="Gurubashi tribe">Gurubashi tribe</a>'s plan to retake <a href="/wiki/Stranglethorn_Vale" title="Stranglethorn Vale">Stranglethorn Vale</a>, leading to a new assault on <a href="/wiki/Zul%27Gurub_(instance)" title="Zul&#39;Gurub (instance)">Zul'Gurub</a>. Their involvement is also touched in Zul'Aman. They were being opposed by <a href="/wiki/Vol%27jin" title="Vol&#39;jin">Vol'jin</a> and his <a href="/wiki/Darkspear" class="mw-redirect" title="Darkspear">Darkspear</a> followers, who claimed that they were manipulating the Amani and were the real threat. Vol'jin explained that the Zandalari were seeking to create a new troll empire, reaching out to all of the troll tribes, including the Amani, Gurubashi, and his own Darkspear tribe - the likes of which would be a grave threat if not immediately dealt with. With aid from both <a href="/wiki/Halduron_Brightwing" title="Halduron Brightwing">Halduron Brightwing</a> and <a href="/wiki/Vereesa_Windrunner" title="Vereesa Windrunner">Vereesa Windrunner</a>, Vol'jin was able to create an appropriate battle plan and personally led a strike force into Zul'Aman and with the aid of adventurers put an end to <a href="/wiki/Daakara" title="Daakara">Daakara</a> and the Zandalari's efforts within Zul'Aman.
</p><p>Darkspear agents inside Zul'Gurub discovered that <a href="/wiki/Jin%27do" title="Jin&#39;do">Jin'do</a> was wrestling <a href="/wiki/Hakkar_the_Soulflayer" title="Hakkar the Soulflayer">Hakkar the Soulflayer</a> into submission, holding him by spirit chains and stripping out his power into himself. They enlisted adventurers to put an end to Jin'do's mad plans once and for all. Upon destroying the spiritual chains holding the Soulflayer in place, Hakkar turned upon Jin'do, and utterly destroyed him, leaving only the corpse of Jin'do the Broken. Hakkar then returned the adventurers to the mortal realm, promising to deal with them another time, before vanishing. Though Hakkar was free, the Zandalari have lost their hold on Zul'Gurub.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Mists_of_Pandaria">Mists of Pandaria</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/wiki/Zandalari_Empire?action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Mists of Pandaria"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div style="clear:left;"></div><p><span style="display:inline-block; min-width: 32px; text-align:center"><a href="/wiki/World_of_Warcraft:_Mists_of_Pandaria" title="Mists of Pandaria"><img alt="Mists of Pandaria" src="/images/Mists-Logo-Small.png?f96b23" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="46" height="14" style="vertical-align: middle" data-file-width="46" data-file-height="14" /></a></span> <span class="greybar" style="font-size: 0.85em; padding: 0 0.2em;"><b>This section concerns content related to <i><a href="/wiki/World_of_Warcraft:_Mists_of_Pandaria" title="World of Warcraft: Mists of Pandaria">Mists of Pandaria</a></i>.</b></span></p><div style="clear:left;"></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:292px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Chronicle_4_Lei_Shen.png" class="image"><img src="/images/thumb/Chronicle_4_Lei_Shen.png/290px-Chronicle_4_Lei_Shen.png?3f4b27" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="290" height="383" class="thumbimage" data-file-width="1920" data-file-height="2539" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Chronicle_4_Lei_Shen.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Prophet_Khar%27zul" title="Prophet Khar&#39;zul">Prophet Khar'zul</a> and the Zandalari resurrecting <a href="/wiki/Lei_Shen" title="Lei Shen">Lei Shen</a> at <a href="/wiki/Thunder%27s_Call" title="Thunder&#39;s Call">Thunder's Call</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>After the <a href="/wiki/Mists_of_Pandaria_(lore)" title="Mists of Pandaria (lore)">mists</a> of <a href="/wiki/Pandaria" title="Pandaria">Pandaria</a> collapsed, the Zandalari were intensely curious about Pandaria, even more so perhaps than the <a href="/wiki/Horde" title="Horde">Horde</a> and <a href="/wiki/Alliance" title="Alliance">Alliance</a> were.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-ZandalariMogu&#95;19-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ZandalariMogu-19"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>19<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Even with their defeat in Zul'Aman and Zul'Gurub, the Zandalari have decided to continue their war efforts and appear in the <a href="/wiki/Kun-Lai_Summit" title="Kun-Lai Summit">Kun-Lai Summit</a> of <a href="/wiki/Pandaria" title="Pandaria">Pandaria</a>. Having set up their base of operations on the island north of the mainland, they now pour down the shores of the region, attacking the denizens. They also hired and brought the <a href="/wiki/Gnoll" title="Gnoll">gnolls</a> of <a href="/wiki/Blackmane_Mercenaries" title="Blackmane Mercenaries">Blackmane</a>.
</p><p>Long before the pandaren established their empire on the continent, the Zandalari were allies with the then-ruling <a href="/wiki/Mogu" title="Mogu">mogu</a> and fought the pandaren for a long time. Now that the mists cloaking the continent have disappeared, the warmongering tribe returns to the ancient continent once more.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>47<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> With their homeland damaged by the <a href="/wiki/Cataclysm" class="mw-redirect" title="Cataclysm">Cataclysm</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>48<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> the Zandalari wish to make Pandaria their new home, before Zandalar sinks beneath the sea. To support them in their campaign, they plan to reforge their old alliance with the mogu by reviving <a href="/wiki/Lei_Shen" title="Lei Shen">Lei Shen</a>, the Thunder King. While King Rastakhan was in support of the plan to unite the lesser troll tribes after the Cataclysm,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>49<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>50<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> he was actually against the plan to revive Lei Shen.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>51<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>The forces of Zandalari in Pandaria are led by <a href="/wiki/Prophet_Khar%27zul" title="Prophet Khar&#39;zul">Prophet Khar'zul</a> under direct orders from the mysterious dark prophet Zul. Most of them are involved in the art of spiritbinding: a common art of dark magic practiced by them and their mogu allies, that can transfer a spirit into a new host body. They plan to reanimate the teracotta bodies slumbering within the crypts of <a href="/wiki/Valley_of_Emperors" title="Valley of Emperors">Valley of Emperors</a> in Kun-Lai Summit in order to fuel the growing mogu army intended to reclaim <a href="/wiki/Pandaria" title="Pandaria">Pandaria</a>, and eventually the dreaded figure that terrorized the land and "lesser races" inhabiting it in ancient times, the infamous emperor <a href="/wiki/Lei_Shen" title="Lei Shen">Lei Shen</a>, the Thunder King himself. The Zandalari eventually manage to reclaim the body and move it to the <a href="/wiki/Isle_of_Reckoning" title="Isle of Reckoning">Isle of Reckoning</a>, where the prophet made his lair. The Zandalari along with the help of all united troll tribes and <a href="/wiki/Mercenary" title="Mercenary">mercenaries</a> started to assault <a href="/wiki/Zouchin_Village" title="Zouchin Village">Zouchin Village</a> near the Summit's coast, but the invasion was ultimately repelled, the prophet slain, but it was too late to stop Thunder King's resurrection.
</p><p>Some of the Zandalari have managed to break into <a href="/wiki/Mogu%27shan_Vaults" title="Mogu&#39;shan Vaults">Mogu'shan Vaults</a>, searching for arcane secrets. <a href="/wiki/Gara%27jal_the_Spiritbinder" title="Gara&#39;jal the Spiritbinder">Gara'jal the Spiritbinder</a> is leading the incursion.
</p>
<h4><span id="Vol.27jin:_Shadows_of_the_Horde"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Vol'jin:_Shadows_of_the_Horde">Vol'jin: Shadows of the Horde</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/wiki/Zandalari_Empire?action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Vol&#039;jin: Shadows of the Horde"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div style="clear:left;"></div><p><span style="display:inline-block; min-width: 32px; text-align:center"><a href="/wiki/Novels" title="Novels"><img alt="WoW-novel-logo-16x62.png" src="/images/WoW-novel-logo-16x62.png?6916a4" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="62" height="16" style="vertical-align: middle" data-file-width="62" data-file-height="16" /></a></span> <span class="greybar" style="font-size: 0.85em; padding: 0 0.2em;"><b>This section concerns content related to the <i>Warcraft</i> <a href="/wiki/Novels" title="Novels">novels</a>, <a href="/wiki/Novellas" title="Novellas">novellas</a>, or <a href="/wiki/Short_stories" title="Short stories">short stories</a>.</b></span></p><div style="clear:left;"></div>
<p>Several weeks after Lei Shen's revival, the Zandalari sent a second attack force to Zouchin Village. Led by <a href="/wiki/Khal%27ak" title="Khal&#39;ak">Khal'ak</a>, the purpose of this attack was to secure a landing spot for Zandalari ships going between Pandaria and the <a href="/wiki/Isle_of_Thunder" title="Isle of Thunder">Isle of Thunder</a>. The attack was met with resistance from the <a href="/wiki/Shado-Pan" title="Shado-Pan">Shado-Pan</a>, as well as <a href="/wiki/Tyrathan_Khort" title="Tyrathan Khort">Tyrathan Khort</a> and Vol'jin. Despite the defense, the Zandalari successfully took Zouchin, and the pandaren living there were forced to flee.
</p><p>Khal'ak and her forces would later travel to <a href="/wiki/Tu_Shen_Burial_Ground" title="Tu Shen Burial Ground">Tu Shen Burial Ground</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Vale_of_Eternal_Blossoms" title="Vale of Eternal Blossoms">Vale of Eternal Blossoms</a> to revive Lei Shen's trusted <a href="/wiki/Warlord_Kao" title="Warlord Kao">Warlord Kao</a>. Once again, they found resistance from the Shado-Pan, though they were taken prisoner by the Zandalari save for Vol'jin, who Khal'ak attempted to convince to leave the <a href="/wiki/Horde" title="Horde">Horde</a> and join the Zandalari. Khal'ak and her forces brought the prisoners and Vol'jin to the Isle of Thunder, where Vol'jin freed his comrades and they returned to the <a href="/wiki/Shado-Pan_Monastery" title="Shado-Pan Monastery">Shado-Pan Monastery</a>.
</p><p>The Zandalari, their mogu allies, and their Gurubashi and Amani servants attacked the Monastery and found themselves facing <a href="/wiki/The_Thirty-three" title="The Thirty-three">The Thirty-three</a>. Despite the overwhelming numbers, the Zandalari forces fell to traps and the superior fighting skills of the Monastery's defenders. Khal'ak was killed when Vol'jin pierced her throat with a <a href="/wiki/Monk" title="Monk">monk</a> technique.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="The_Thunder_King">The Thunder King</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/wiki/Zandalari_Empire?action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: The Thunder King"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div style="clear:left;"></div><p><span style="display:inline-block; min-width: 32px; text-align:center"><a href="/wiki/World_of_Warcraft:_Mists_of_Pandaria" title="Mists of Pandaria"><img alt="Mists of Pandaria" src="/images/Mists-Logo-Small.png?f96b23" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="46" height="14" style="vertical-align: middle" data-file-width="46" data-file-height="14" /></a></span> <span class="greybar" style="font-size: 0.85em; padding: 0 0.2em;"><b>This section concerns content related to <i><a href="/wiki/World_of_Warcraft:_Mists_of_Pandaria" title="World of Warcraft: Mists of Pandaria">Mists of Pandaria</a></i>.</b></span></p><div style="clear:left;"></div>
<p>Most of the Zandalari supporting <a href="/wiki/Zul" title="Zul">Zul</a> have joined Lei Shen in his island fortress - <a href="/wiki/Throne_of_Thunder" title="Throne of Thunder">Throne of Thunder</a>. Under Lei Shen's orders, the Zandalari begin building massive ships in areas already under mogu occupation. Zandalari and mogu forces have landed at most of the coastlines in Pandaria and have begun to invade the surrounding regions. This Zandalari army that marched on the island of the Thunder King is not the entire Zandalari race, but one branch of their force that's cruising out around the world.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Patch&#95;5.2.0&#95;52-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patch_5.2.0-52"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>52<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> The Zandalari were promised generous holdings along the northern coast of Pandaria by Lei Shen.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>53<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Sometime after the events at the <a href="/wiki/Isle_of_Thunder" title="Isle of Thunder">Isle of Thunder</a>, the Zandalari had turned their focus on rallying the <a href="/wiki/Frostmane_tribe" title="Frostmane tribe">Frostmane tribe</a> of <a href="/wiki/Dun_Morogh" title="Dun Morogh">Dun Morogh</a> against <a href="/wiki/Ironforge" title="Ironforge">Ironforge</a>, but their plans were thwarted by <a href="/wiki/Moira_Bronzebeard" class="mw-redirect" title="Moira Bronzebeard">Moira Bronzebeard</a> and <a href="/wiki/Varian_Wrynn" title="Varian Wrynn">Varian Wrynn</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>54<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Legion">Legion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/wiki/Zandalari_Empire?action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Legion"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div style="clear:left;"></div><p><span style="display:inline-block; min-width: 32px; text-align:center"><a href="/wiki/World_of_Warcraft:_Legion" title="Legion"><img alt="Legion" src="/images/Legion-Logo-Small.png?7a5440" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="40" height="15" style="vertical-align: middle" data-file-width="40" data-file-height="15" /></a></span> <span class="greybar" style="font-size: 0.85em; padding: 0 0.2em;"><b>This section concerns content related to <i><a href="/wiki/World_of_Warcraft:_Legion" title="World of Warcraft: Legion">Legion</a></i>.</b></span></p><div style="clear:left;"></div>
<p>A few Zandalari have shown up among the <a href="/wiki/Shipwrecked_Captive" title="Shipwrecked Captive">shipwrecked captives</a> in <a href="/wiki/Oceanus_Cove" title="Oceanus Cove">Oceanus Cove</a> in <a href="/wiki/Azsuna" title="Azsuna">Azsuna</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Battle_for_Azeroth">Battle for Azeroth</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/wiki/Zandalari_Empire?action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Battle for Azeroth"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div style="clear:left;"></div><p><span style="display:inline-block; min-width: 32px; text-align:center"><a href="/wiki/World_of_Warcraft:_Battle_for_Azeroth" title="Battle for Azeroth"><img alt="Battle for Azeroth" src="/images/thumb/BattleForAzeroth-Logo-Small.png/40px-BattleForAzeroth-Logo-Small.png?25e36c" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="40" height="15" style="vertical-align: middle" data-file-width="80" data-file-height="29" /></a></span> <span class="greybar" style="font-size: 0.85em; padding: 0 0.2em;"><b>This section concerns content related to <i><a href="/wiki/World_of_Warcraft:_Battle_for_Azeroth" title="World of Warcraft: Battle for Azeroth">Battle for Azeroth</a></i>.</b></span></p><div style="clear:left;"></div>
<p>At an unknown point in time, <a href="/wiki/Warchief" title="Warchief">Warchief</a> <a href="/wiki/Sylvanas_Windrunner" title="Sylvanas Windrunner">Sylvanas Windrunner</a> entered into negotiations with <a href="/wiki/Princess_Talanji" class="mw-redirect" title="Princess Talanji">Princess Talanji</a> of the Zandalari Empire. The Warchief desired the might of the <a href="/wiki/Golden_Fleet" title="Golden Fleet">Zandalari navy</a>, while Talanji sought the strength of Horde champions to combat the threats within <a href="/wiki/Zandalar" title="Zandalar">Zandalar</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>55<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> However, Talanji was captured by the Alliance while en route to Orgrimmar. Catching wind of this, Sylvanas ordered a strike team to liberate her from the <a href="/wiki/Stockades" class="mw-redirect" title="Stockades">Stockades</a> and deliver her to <a href="/wiki/Orgrimmar" title="Orgrimmar">Orgrimmar</a>. When freed, Talanji instead decided to return to <a href="/wiki/Zandalar" title="Zandalar">Zandalar</a> with the Horde forces sent to retrieve her.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>56<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> For rescuing his daughter, <a href="/wiki/King_Rastakhan" class="mw-redirect" title="King Rastakhan">King Rastakhan</a> allowed the Horde to set up an embassy within the <a href="/wiki/Great_Seal" title="Great Seal">Great Seal</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>57<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Unbeknownst to Rastakhan, <a href="/wiki/Prophet_Zul" class="mw-redirect" title="Prophet Zul">Prophet Zul</a> had pledged allegiance to the <a href="/wiki/Old_God" title="Old God">Old God</a> <a href="/wiki/G%27huun" title="G&#39;huun">G'huun</a> and sought to overthrow him and create a new troll empire with G'huun as its god.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>58<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Thus, he triggered a <a href="/wiki/War_in_Zandalar" title="War in Zandalar">civil war</a> between <a href="/wiki/Atal%27zul" title="Atal&#39;zul">his followers</a> and Rastakhan's loyalists, during which he killed and revived <a href="/wiki/Rezan" title="Rezan">Rezan</a> with his dark power. With Rezan's death, Rastakhan began aging fast.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>59<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Meanwhile, Talanji led <a href="/wiki/Talanji%27s_Expedition" title="Talanji&#39;s Expedition">an expedition</a> into the swamps of <a href="/wiki/Nazmir" title="Nazmir">Nazmir</a> in order to combat the <a href="/wiki/Blood_troll" title="Blood troll">blood trolls</a> that assaulted <a href="/wiki/Zuldazar" title="Zuldazar">Zuldazar</a>, where they discovered the existence of G'huun within <a href="/wiki/Uldir" title="Uldir">Uldir</a> and killed the blood troll leader, <a href="/wiki/Grand_Ma%27da_Ateena" title="Grand Ma&#39;da Ateena">Grand Ma'da Ateena</a>. Despite Ateena's death, the blood trolls remained a threat and successfully launched an attack on <a href="/wiki/Zuldazar" title="Zuldazar">Zuldazar</a>, demoralizing Rastakhan. Determined to save the Zandalari Empire, Rastakhan turned to <a href="/wiki/Bwonsamdi" title="Bwonsamdi">Bwonsamdi</a> and struck a deal: If Bwonsamdi gave him the power to take back his empire, then Rastakhan would elevate Bwonsamdi above all other loa in the Zandalari pantheon. Bwonsamdi accepted the deal on one condition: that the king's bloodline would be devoted to Bwonsamdi, to serve him in life and in death. Sometime after this, Zul led his forces in an assault on <a href="/wiki/Dazar%27alor" title="Dazar&#39;alor">Dazar'alor</a>, accompanied by the <a href="/wiki/C%27Thrax" title="C&#39;Thrax">C'Thrax</a> warbringer <a href="/wiki/Mythrax" title="Mythrax">Mythrax</a>. While the Zandalari Empire and their allies were ultimately able to defeat Zul and his men, with Rastakhan personally killing Zul, Mythrax was able to break the final seal holding G'huun.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>60<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>In time, <a href="/wiki/Adventurer" title="Adventurer">champions</a> ventured into Uldir and killed G'huun, with Talanji presenting their victory to the Zandalari people in the aftermath.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>61<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Joining_the_Horde">Joining the Horde</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/wiki/Zandalari_Empire?action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Joining the Horde"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div style="clear:left;"></div><p><span style="display:inline-block; min-width: 32px; text-align:center"><a href="/wiki/World_of_Warcraft:_Battle_for_Azeroth" title="Battle for Azeroth"><img alt="Battle for Azeroth" src="/images/thumb/BattleForAzeroth-Logo-Small.png/40px-BattleForAzeroth-Logo-Small.png?25e36c" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="40" height="15" style="vertical-align: middle" data-file-width="80" data-file-height="29" /></a></span> <span class="greybar" style="font-size: 0.85em; padding: 0 0.2em;"><b>This section concerns content related to <i><a href="/wiki/World_of_Warcraft:_Battle_for_Azeroth" title="World of Warcraft: Battle for Azeroth">Battle for Azeroth</a></i>.</b></span></p><div style="clear:left;"></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:322px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Allegiance_of_the_Zandalari.png" class="image"><img src="/images/thumb/Allegiance_of_the_Zandalari.png/320px-Allegiance_of_the_Zandalari.png?20f0f5" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="320" height="221" class="thumbimage" data-file-width="1562" data-file-height="1079" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Allegiance_of_the_Zandalari.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Talanji" title="Talanji">Queen Talanji</a> pledging her people to <a href="/wiki/Warchief" title="Warchief">Warchief</a> <a href="/wiki/Sylvanas_Windrunner" title="Sylvanas Windrunner">Sylvanas Windrunner</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Horde" title="Horde">Horde</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>After receiving word that the <a href="/wiki/Alliance" title="Alliance">Alliance</a> had mounted an attack through <a href="/wiki/Nazmir" title="Nazmir">Nazmir</a>, the Zandalari Empire determined that while Rastakhan would defend the city, Talanji would lead the armies of the Zandalari Empire against the Alliance. However, the attack through Nazmir proved to be a ruse to lure away the Zandalari and Horde armies from Dazar'alor, leaving the city defenses weakened enough for the Alliance to <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Dazar%27alor" title="Battle of Dazar&#39;alor">invade</a> the city from the <a href="/wiki/Port_of_Zandalar" title="Port of Zandalar">Port of Zandalar</a>.
